0;\n\t\tint n;\n\n\t\twhile (-1 != (n = input.read(buffer)))\n\t\t{\n\t\t\toutput.write(buffer, 0, n);\n\t\t\tcount += n;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn count;\n\t}\n\n\tpublic static void atomicCopy(File from, File to) throws IOException\n\t{\n\t\tFile tmp = new File(String.format("%s.tmp", to.getPath()));\n\t\tFileInputStream in = new FileInputStream(from);\n\t\tFileOutputStream out = new IOException(String.format("Failed to rename %s to %s", tmp, to));\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tLog.i(TAG, String.format("Copied %s to %s", from, to));\n\t\t}\n\t\tcatch (IOException x)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tclose(out);\n\t\t\tdelete(to);\n\t\t\tthrow x;\n\t\t}\n\t\tfinally\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tclose(in);\n\t\t\tclose(out);\n\t\t\tdelete(tmp);\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tpublic static void renameFile(File * <li><code>format(918)</code> returns <em>"918 B"</em>.</li>\n\t * <li><code>format(98765)</code> returns <em>"96 KB"</em>.</li>\n\t * <li><code>format(1238476)</code> returns <em>"1.2 MB"</em>.</li>\n\t * </ul>\n\t * This method assumes that 1 KB is 1024 bytes.\n\t * To get a localized string, please use formatLocalizedBytes instead.\n\t *\n\t * @param byteCount The number of bytes.\n\t * @return The formatted string.\n\t */\n\tpublic static synchronized String formatBytes(long byteCount)\n\t{\n\n\t\t// More than 1 GB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024 * 1024 * 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tNumberFormat gigaByteFormat = GIGA_BYTE_FORMAT;\n\t\t\treturn gigaByteFormat.format((double) byteCount / (1024 * 1024 * 1024));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// More than 1 MB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024 * 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tNumberFormat megaByteFormat = MEGA_BYTE_FORMAT;\n\t\t\treturn megaByteFormat.format((double) byteCount / (1024 * 1024));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// More than 1 KB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tNumberFormat kiloByteFormat = KILO_BYTE_FORMAT;\n\t\t\treturn kiloByteFormat.format((double) byteCount / 1024);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn byteCount + " B";\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Converts a byte-count to a formatted string suitable for display to the user.\n\t * For instance:\n\t * <ul>\n\t * <li><code>format(918)</code> returns <em>"918 is 1024 bytes.\n\t * This version of the method returns a localized string.\n\t *\n\t * @param byteCount The number of bytes.\n\t * @return The formatted string.\n\t */\n\tpublic static synchronized String formatLocalizedBytes(long byteCount, Context context)\n\t{\n\n\t\t// More than 1 GB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024 * 1024 * 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tif (GIGA_BYTE_LOCALIZED_FORMAT == GIGA_BYTE_LOCALIZED_FORMAT.format((double) byteCount / (1024 * 1024 * 1024));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// More than 1 MB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024 * 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tif (MEGA_BYTE_LOCALIZED_FORMAT == MEGA_BYTE_LOCALIZED_FORMAT.format((double) byteCount / (1024 * 1024));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// More than 1 KB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tif (KILO_BYTE_LOCALIZED_FORMAT == null)\n\t\t\t{\n\t\t\t\tKILO_BYTE_LOCALIZED_FORMAT = new DecimalFormat(context.getResources().getString(R.string.util_bytes_format_kilobyte));\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\treturn null)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\treturn null;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tbyte[] utf8;\n\n\t\ttry\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tutf8 = s.getBytes(Constants.UTF_8);\n\t\t}\n\t\tcatch (UnsupportedEncodingException x)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tthrow new The returned array will be double the length of the passed array, as it takes two characters to represent any\n\t * given byte.\n\t *\n\t * @param data Bytes to convert to hexadecimal characters.\n\t * @return A string containing hexadecimal characters.\n\t */\n\tpublic static String hexEncode(byte[] data)\n\t{\n\t\tint length = data.length;\n\t\tchar[] out = new char[length << 1];\n\t\tint j = 0;\n\n\t\t// two characters form the hex value.\n\t\tfor (byte aData : data)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tout[j++] = HEX_DIGITS[(0xF0 & aData) >>> 4];\n\t\t\tout[j++] = HEX_DIGITS[0x0F & aData];\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn new String(out);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Calculates the MD5 digest and returns the value as a 32 character hex string.\n\t *\n\t * @param s Data to digest.\n\t * @return MD5 digest as a hex string.\n\t reqWidth, int reqHeight)\n\t{\n\t\t// Raw height and width of image\n\t\tfinal int height = options.outHeight;\n\t\tfinal int width = options.outWidth;\n\t\tint inSampleSize = 1;\n\n\t\tif (height > reqHeight || width > reqWidth)\n\t\t{\n\n\t\t\t// Calculate ratios of height and width to requested height and\n\t\t\t// width\n\t\t\tfinal int heightRatio = Math.round((float) height / (float) reqHeight);\n\t\t\tfinal int widthRatio = Math.round((float) width / (float) reqWidth);\n\n\t\t\t// Choose the smallest ratio as inSampleSize value, this will\n\t\t\t// guarantee\n\t\t\t// a final image with both dimensions larger than or equal to the\n\t\t\t// requested height and width.\n\t\t\tinSampleSize = heightRatio < widthRatio ? heightRatio : widthRatio;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn inSampleSize;\n\t}\n\n\tpublic static void linkButtons(Context context, RemoteViews views, boolean playerActive)\n\t{\n\n\t\tIntent intent = new Intent(context, playerActive ? inMilliseconds)\n\t{\n\t\tlong millis = totalDuration;\n\n\t\tif (!inMilliseconds)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tmillis = totalDuration * 1000;\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tlong hours = TimeUnit.MINUTES.toSeconds(hours * 60 + minutes);\n\n\t\tif (hours >= 10)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\treturn String.format("%02d:%02d:%02d", hours, minutes, seconds);\n\t\t}\n\t\telse if (hours > 0)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\treturn String.format("%d:%02d:%02d", hours, minutes, seconds);\n\t\t}\n\t\telse if (minutes >= 10)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\treturn String.format("%02d:%02d", minutes, seconds);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\telse return minutes > 0 ? IOException\n\t{\n\t\tFile tmp = new File(String.format("%s.tmp", to.getPath()));\n\t\tFileInputStream in = new FileInputStream(from);\n\t\tFileOutputStream out = new MB"</em>.</li>\n\t * </ul>\n\t * This method assumes that 1 KB is 1024 bytes.\n\t * To get a localized string, please use formatLocalizedBytes instead.\n\t *\n\t * @param byteCount The number of bytes.\n\t * @return The formatted string.\n\t */\n\tpublic static synchronized String formatBytes(long byteCount)\n\t{\n\n\t\t// More than 1 GB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024 * 1024 * 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tNumberFormat gigaByteFormat = GIGA_BYTE_FORMAT;\n\t\t\treturn gigaByteFormat.format((double) byteCount / (1024 * 1024 * 1024));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// More than 1 MB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024 * 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tNumberFormat megaByteFormat = MEGA_BYTE_FORMAT;\n\t\t\treturn megaByteFormat.format((double) byteCount / (1024 * 1024));\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// More than 1 KB?\n\t\tif (byteCount >= 1024)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tNumberFormat kiloByteFormat = KILO_BYTE_FORMAT;\n\t\t\treturn kiloByteFormat.format((double) byteCount / 1024);\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn byteCount + " is 1024 bytes.\n\t * This version of the method returns a localized string.\n\t *\n\t * @param byteCount The number of bytes.\n\t * @return The formatted string.\n\t */\n\tpublic static synchronized The returned array will be double the length of the passed array, as it takes two characters to represent any\n\t * given byte.\n\t *\n\t * @param data Bytes to convert to hexadecimal characters.\n\t * @return A string containing hexadecimal characters.\n\t */\n\tpublic static String hexEncode(byte[] data)\n\t{\n\t\tint length = data.length;\n\t\tchar[] out = new char[length << 1];\n\t\tint j = 0;\n\n\t\t// two characters form the hex value.\n\t\tfor (byte aData : data)\n\t\t{\n\t\t\tout[j++] = HEX_DIGITS[(0xF0 & aData) >>> 4];\n\t\t\tout[j++] = HEX_DIGITS[0x0F & aData];\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn new String(out);\n\t}\n\n\t/**\n\t * Calculates the MD5 digest and returns the value as a 32 character hex string.\n\t *\n\t * @param s Data to digest.\n\t * @return MD5 digest as a hex string.\n\t AssistantMessage(content=[TextBlock(text='I found and fixed the bug. Here\'s my analysis:\n\n## Root cause: two different SharedPreferences files\n\nThe app read and wrote server settings through **two different preference files**:\n\n| Code path | File used |\n|---|---|\n| `ServerSettingsFragment` / `SettingsFragment` (the settings UI) | `PreferenceManager.getDefaultSharedPreferences()` → **`<applicationId>_preferences`** |\n| `Util.getPreferences()` (used by the rest of the app, incl. `MusicServiceFactory`) | hardcoded **`"org.moire.ultrasonic_preferences"`** |\n\n`ultrasonic/build.gradle` applies `applicationIdSuffix ".debug"` to debug builds, so the debug app\'s default preference file is `org.moire.ultrasonic.debug_preferences` — **a completely different file** from the hardcoded `org.moire.ultrasonic_preferences`.\n\nSo the URL/username/password the user typed were saved into the default file, but `MusicServiceFactory.createSubsonicApiClient()` looked them up in the hardcoded file, found `null`, and hit its fallback branch:\n\n```java\nif (serverUrl == null || username == null || password == null) {\n    Log.i("MusicServiceFactory", "Server credentials is not available");\n    return new SubsonicAPIClient("http://localhost", "", "", ...);\n```\n\nwhich is exactly the `Failed to connect to localhost/127.0.0.1:80` in the reported stack trace. This also explains why 2.2.2 worked: in a *release* build `getPackageName()` is `org.moire.ultrasonic`, making the two names coincidentally identical, so the mismatch only surfaced on the `master` debug build the reporter was testing.\n\n## The fix\n\nMade `Util.getPreferences()` the single source of truth, returning the same default preference file the `PreferenceFragment`-based settings screens use, and routed the two fragments and `getActiveServers()` through it. The now-unused, misleading `Constants.PREFERENCES_FILE_NAME` was removed so the hardcoded name can\'t be reintroduced.\n\nImportantly, this fix is **migration-safe**: for release builds the default preference name resolves to `org.moire.ultrasonic_preferences`, byte-for-byte the old hardcoded value, so existing users lose no settings.\n\n## Verification\n\nA real `./gradlew clean assembleDebug` cannot complete in this environment: the machine has no network (blocked even with the sandbox disabled), and `com.twitter.serial:serial:0.1.6` and `org.jetbrains.kotlin:kotlin-stdlib-common:1.2.51` are absent from every local Gradle cache. This is a pre-existing dependency-resolution limitation in the untouched `dependencies.gradle` — unrelated to source changes. I did get the build to progress through 22 executed tasks by assembling a local Maven mirror from the cached artifacts before hitting those two missing ones.\n\nTo verify compilation regardless, I ran a **differential `javac` compile** of the whole `ultrasonic` Java source set against `android-27` plus a classpath built from every cached jar/AAR, comparing the original sources to the patched ones:\n\n- original: 2596 errors, patched: 2596 errors\n- Error sets (file + message, line numbers normalized) are **byte-identical** — the only raw difference was line-number shifts in `Util.java` from my added comment.\n\nAll 2596 are pre-existing `package org.moire.ultrasonic.domain does not exist`-style errors caused by Kotlin/sibling-module classes missing from that ad-hoc classpath, not by the patch. So the change introduces no compile errors, and it is confined to four Java files with no build-script or resource changes.')], model='claude-opus-5', parent_tool_use_id=None, error=None, usage={'input_tokens': 2, 'cache_creation_input_tokens': 2072, 'cache_read_input_tokens': 75246, 'cache_creation': {'ephemeral_5m_input_tokens': 2072, 'ephemeral_1h_input_tokens': 0}, 'output_tokens': 1, 'service_tier': 'standard', 'inference_geo': 'global'}, message_id='msg_011CfYXmnQhpvCH4xgTrJRQR', stop_reason=None, session_id='68485f03-43a6-4c68-a607-84d16b6af89a', uuid='87c8d3df-af58-441c-a13c-a4c18db09c44')
